E_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\pm}}[V_{d}\left(\mathbf{R}\right)]=\pm\varepsilon_{0}+W\left\langle\Psi_{0}^{\pm}\right|\hat{V}_{d}\left|\Psi_{0}^{\pm}\right\rangle-W^{2}\!\!\!\!\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in F\!B\!Z}\sum_{s=\pm}\frac{\left\langle\Psi_{0}^{\pm}\right|\hat{V}_{d}\left|\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}^{s}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}^{s}\right|\hat{V}_{d}\left|\Psi_{0}^{\pm}\right\rangle}{\varepsilon^{s}(\mathbf{k})\mp\varepsilon_{0}},

(598)

such that
\mathcal{H}_{0}\left|\Psi_{0}^{\pm}\right\rangle\!=\!\pm\varepsilon_{0}\left|\Psi_{0}^{\pm}\right\rangle.

The previous summation over
\mathbf{k}
excludes the lowest available
momentum,
\mathbf{k}\!=\!2\pi\boldsymbol{\varphi}/L
, where
\boldsymbol{\varphi}

is the vector containing the boundary phase-twists applied to the
system [see Sect. (
19
) for context],
and
s
there stands for the band index.

Equation (
598
) basically defines the energy
of the two non-degenerate levels closest to the node as a function
of the random field
V_{d}(\mathbf{R})
. This implies that
E_{0}^{\pm}

will also be a random variable that inherits the statistical properties
of the disordered landscape. We will now characterize their statistics
by measuring the mean and standard-deviation for a finite sample of
size
L
. Before anything, we note that, by construction

\left|\Psi_{0}^{\pm}\right\rangle\!=\!\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\frac{e^{i2\pi\boldsymbol{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{R}/L}}{L^{\frac{3}{2}}}\left|\mathbf{R},\pm\right\rangle\quad;\left|\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}^{s}\right\rangle\!=\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\frac{e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{R}}}{L^{\frac{3}{2}}}\left|\mathbf{R},s\right\rangle

(599)

and, since the disorder is assumed to act as a scalar within a unit-cell,
we have

\left\langle\Psi_{0}^{\pm}\right|\hat{V}_{d}\left|\Psi_{0}^{\pm}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{L^{3}}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{Q}}e^{i2\pi\boldsymbol{\varphi}\cdot(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{Q})/L}\left\langle\mathbf{Q},\pm\right|\hat{V}_{d}\left|\mathbf{R},\pm\right\rangle=\frac{1}{L^{3}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}V(\mathbf{R})

(600)

and, likewise,

\displaystyle\left\langle\Psi_{0}^{\pm}\right|\hat{V}_{d}\left|\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}^{s}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}^{s}\right|\hat{V}_{d}\left|\Psi_{0}^{\pm}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L^{6}}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{Q}}\sum_{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{S}}\sum_{s=\pm}e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{P})}e^{i2\pi\boldsymbol{\varphi}\cdot(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{S})/L}\times

(601)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times V_{d}(\mathbf{R})V_{d}(\mathbf{Q})\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{P}}\delta_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{S}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L^{6}}\sum_{s=\pm}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{Q}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}-2\pi\boldsymbol{\varphi}/L)\cdot(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{R})}V_{d}(\mathbf{R})V_{d}(\mathbf{Q}),

and finally we get to

\displaystyle E_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\pm}}[V_{d}\left(\mathbf{R}\right)]

\displaystyle=\pm\varepsilon_{0}+\frac{W}{L^{3}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}V(\mathbf{R})-\frac{W^{2}}{L^{6}}\!\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{Q}}V_{d}(\mathbf{R})V_{d}(\mathbf{Q})\times

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in F\!B\!Z}\frac{e^{i(\mathbf{k}-2\pi\boldsymbol{\varphi}/L)\cdot(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{R})}}{\varepsilon^{+}(\mathbf{k})\mp\varepsilon_{0}}+\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in F\!B\!Z}\frac{e^{i(\mathbf{k}-2\pi\boldsymbol{\varphi}/L)\cdot(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{R})}}{\varepsilon^{-}(\mathbf{k})\mp\varepsilon_{0}}\right],

(602)

which is the explicit formula of the functional. Considering only
the case
+
only, we get

\displaystyle E_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle+}}[V_{d}\left(\mathbf{R}\right)]

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{0}+\frac{W}{L^{3}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}V(\mathbf{R})-\frac{W^{2}}{L^{6}}\!\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{Q}}V_{d}(\mathbf{R})V_{d}(\mathbf{Q})\times

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in F\!B\!Z^{\prime}}\frac{e^{i(\mathbf{k}-2\pi\boldsymbol{\varphi}/L)\cdot(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{R})}}{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}-\varepsilon_{0}}-\!\!\!\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in F\!B\!Z}\frac{e^{i(\mathbf{k}-2\pi\boldsymbol{\varphi}/L)\cdot(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{R})}}{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}+\varepsilon_{0}}\right],

(603)

By definition, we will be considering only samples for which the summation
over the entire lattice is zero. Hence, the functional gets an even
simpler form,

\displaystyle E_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle+}}[V_{d}\left(\mathbf{R}\right)]

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{0}-\frac{W^{2}}{L^{6}}\!\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{Q}}V_{d}(\mathbf{R})V_{d}(\mathbf{Q})\times

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left[\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in F\!B\!Z^{\prime}}\frac{e^{i(\mathbf{k}-2\pi\boldsymbol{\varphi}/L)\cdot(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{R})}}{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}-\varepsilon_{0}}-\!\!\!\!\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in F\!B\!Z}\frac{e^{i(\mathbf{k}-2\pi\boldsymbol{\varphi}/L)\cdot(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{R})}}{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}+\varepsilon_{0}}\right]

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{0}-\frac{W^{2}}{L^{6}}\!\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{Q}}\!\!V_{d}(\mathbf{R})V_{d}(\mathbf{Q})\left[\!\!\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in F\!B\!Z^{\prime}}\left(\!\frac{2\varepsilon_{0}e^{i(\mathbf{k}-2\pi\boldsymbol{\varphi}/L)\cdot(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{R})}}{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}\!\right)-\frac{1}{\Delta_{\text{gap}}^{L}}\!\right]

(604)

Now, we can evaluate the average over disorder realizations,

i.e.
,

\displaystyle\left\langle E_{0}^{+}\right\rangle_{d}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{0}+\frac{W^{2}}{L^{3}}\left[\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in F\!B\!Z^{\prime}}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}-\varepsilon_{0}}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}+\varepsilon_{0}}\right)-\frac{1}{\Delta_{\text{gap}}^{L}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad=\varepsilon_{0}-\frac{W^{2}}{L^{3}}\left[\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in F\!B\!Z^{\prime}}\left(\frac{2\varepsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}\right)-\frac{1}{\Delta_{\text{gap}}^{L}}\right]

(605)

where we have put aside the coupling between the two lowest-lying
states. In the limit
L\to\infty
, we can see that

\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in F\!B\!Z^{\prime}}\left(\frac{2\varepsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}\right)\sim L^{2}\quad;\text{ and }\frac{1}{\Delta_{\text{gap}}^{L}}\sim L,

(606)

which means that the leading correction to the average value of the
first energy level will be

\left\langle E_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\pm}}\right\rangle_{d}=\pm\varepsilon_{0}\mp\mathcal{K}_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}\frac{W^{2}}{L}+\mathcal{O}[\frac{1}{L^{2}}]

(607)

This expression indicates what will be the average shift caused by
the disorder on the two levels closest to the Weyl node. Note that
both levels are equally drawn towards the node by the effect of disorder
and, in addition, the shift is proportional to
1/L
. Since
\varepsilon_{0}

is also proportional to
1/L
, we conclude that the size of the finite
size gap is renormalized by disorder as follows:

\frac{\Delta_{\text{gap}}^{d}\!(L)-\Delta_{\text{gap}}^{0,L}\!(L)}{\Delta_{\text{gap}}^{0,L}\!(L)}=-\mathcal{K}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\frac{W^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}L}\propto L^{0}.

(608)

From now on, we will always disregard de
1/\Delta_{\text{gap}}^{L}
,
which is always non-leading in
1/L
. Meanwhile, an analogous analysis
can be done to obtain the width of the distribution for this first
level. For this, we calculate the squared-average of
E_{0}^{\pm}
,
that is

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\left\langle\left(E_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle+}}\right)^{2}\right\rangle_{d}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{0}^{2}-4\varepsilon_{0}\frac{W^{2}}{L^{6}}\!\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{Q}}\left\langle V_{d}(\mathbf{R})V_{d}(\mathbf{Q})\right\rangle_{d}\left[\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in F\!B\!Z^{\prime}}\left(\frac{2\varepsilon_{0}e^{i(\mathbf{k}-2\pi\boldsymbol{\theta}/L)\cdot(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{R})}}{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{16W^{4}}{L^{12}}\!\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{Q}}\sum_{\mathbf{L},\mathbf{S}}\left\langle V_{d}(\mathbf{R})V_{d}(\mathbf{Q})V_{d}(\mathbf{L})V_{d}(\mathbf{S})\right\rangle_{d}\times

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left[\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q}\in F\!B\!Z^{\prime}}\left[\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\right)\left(\varepsilon_{\mathbf{q}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\right)}\right]e^{i(\mathbf{k}-2\pi\boldsymbol{\theta}/L)\cdot(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{R})}e^{i(\mathbf{q}-2\pi\boldsymbol{\theta}/L)\cdot(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{R})}\right]

(609)

and, now, we can make use of the statistical properties of
V(\mathbf{R})
,
given in Eqs. (
596
)-(
597
), which yields a total
of five non-zero terms,
i.e
.,

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\left\langle\left(E_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle+}}\right)^{2}\right\rangle_{d}\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{0}^{2}-8\varepsilon_{0}\frac{W^{2}}{L^{3}}\!\left[\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in F\!B\!Z^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}\right)\right]\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{16W^{4}}{L^{6}}\!\left[\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q}\in F\!B\!Z^{\prime}}\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\right)\left(\varepsilon_{\mathbf{q}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{16W^{4}}{L^{12}}\!\left[\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q}\in F\!B\!Z^{\prime}}\left[\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\right)\left(\varepsilon_{\mathbf{q}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\right)}\right]\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{Q}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q})\cdot(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{R})}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{16W^{4}}{L^{12}}\!\left[\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q}\in F\!B\!Z^{\prime}}\left[\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\right)\left(\varepsilon_{\mathbf{q}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\right)}\right]\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{Q}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}-4\pi\boldsymbol{\varphi}/L)\cdot(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{R})}\right].

(610)

Now, the first three terms can be identified as
\left\langle E_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\pm}}\right\rangle_{d}^{2}
,
meaning that the variance of
E_{0}^{\pm}
reads simply

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\left\langle\left(E_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\pm}}\right)^{2}\right\rangle_{d}

\displaystyle\!\!\!-\!\left\langle E_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\pm}}\right\rangle_{d}^{2}=\frac{32W^{4}}{L^{12}}\!\left[\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q}\in F\!B\!Z^{\prime}}\left[\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\right)\left(\varepsilon_{\mathbf{q}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\right)}\right]\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{Q}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q})\cdot(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{R})}\right],\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!

(611)

which, finally yields

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(E_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\pm}}\right)^{2}\right\rangle_{d}\!\!-\left\langle E_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\pm}}\right\rangle_{d}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4W^{4}}{L^{6}}\!\!\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in F\!B\!Z^{\prime}}\left[\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}}\right].

(612)

Once again, we can show that

\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in F\!B\!Z^{\prime}}\left[\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}}\right]\sim L^{2},

(613)

meaning that

\left\langle\left(E_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\pm}}\right)^{2}\right\rangle_{d}-\left\langle E_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\pm}}\right\rangle_{d}^{2}\propto\left(\frac{W}{L}\right)^{4},

(614)

which yields a broadening that goes as
W^{2}\!/L^{2}
, in perturbation
theory.
